4 9. Emergency funding for nonprofit food banks. During a state of emergency
5 declared in accordance with Title 37‑B, section 742, subsection 1 that exceeds 10 days, the

7 Conservation and Forestry, may distribute up to $400,000 $4,000,000 from the stabilization

9 provide or distribute food to low-income, indigent or unemployed individuals or
10 households without charge. The Commissioner of Agriculture, Conservation and Forestry
11 shall adopt rules to prescribe the manner in which the funds distributed under this

13 this subsection to match any relevant federal funding available for qualifying food
14 programs in the State. Rules adopted pursuant to this subsection are routine technical rules

16 37‑B, section 742, subsection 1, paragraph C, subparagraph (1) rules adopted under this
17 subsection.

19 10. Emergency heating assistance. During a state of emergency declared in
20 accordance with Title 37-B, section 742, subsection 1 that exceeds 10 days, the Governor,
21 in coordination with the Maine State Housing Authority, may distribute up to $1,500,000
22 from the stabilization fund to the Maine State Housing Authority without charge to be used
23 for the administration of benefit payments for emergency heating assistance. The Maine
24 State Housing Authority shall return any of the funds distributed pursuant to this subsection

26 Housing Authority must be deposited to the stabilization fund.
27 Sec. F-3. 5 MRSA §1532, sub-§11 is enacted to read:
28 11. Emergency nutrition assistance. During a state of emergency declared in

36 subsection if federal funding becomes available at a later time. Any funds returned by the

23 §683-A. Homestead exemption
24 1. Homestead property tax exemption; generally. For property tax years beginning

13 Sec. N-3. 36 MRSA c. 815, sub-c. 3 is enacted to read:
14 SUBCHAPTER 3
15 PASS-THROUGH ENTITY TAX
16 §5199-A. Definitions
17 As used in this subchapter, unless the context otherwise indicates, the following terms
18 have the following meanings.
19 1. Allowable credits. "Allowable credits" means the total amount of any payments
20 with regard to a taxpayer that have been or will be paid to the bureau prior to the date the
21 payment against which the total amount of any payments is to be used as a credit is due
22 and that are available to offset any estimated tax liability under section 5199-H.
23 2. Distributive share of income. "Distributive share of income" means, with regard
24 to a qualified member that is a resident individual or resident estate or trust, the net of the
25 portion of income, gain, loss and deduction for the taxable year from the pass-through
26 entity, including guaranteed payments, entering into the qualified member's federal
27 adjusted gross income in the case of a resident individual or federal taxable income in the
28 case of a resident estate or trust. "Distributive share of income" means, with regard to a
29 qualified member that is a nonresident individual or nonresident estate or trust, the net of
30 the portion of income, gain, loss and deduction derived from or connected with sources in
31 this State, as provided by section 5142, for the taxable year from the pass-through entity,
32 including guaranteed payments, entering into the qualified member's federal adjusted gross
33 income in the case of a nonresident individual or federal taxable income in the case of a
34 nonresident estate or trust. If the qualified member is a trust, residency is determined by
35 the residency status of the trust and not by the residency status of the beneficiaries of the
36 trust. For purposes of this subchapter, the distributive share of income for a qualified
37 member is zero if the amount determined in accordance with this subsection is zero or less.
38 3. Distributive share of tax. "Distributive share of tax" means the tax paid by an
39 electing pass-through entity pursuant to section 5199-C multiplied by a fraction, the
40 numerator of which is equal to the qualified member's distributive share of income and the
41 denominator of which is equal to the pass-through entity taxable income.
Page 149 - 132LR3049(02)
COMMITTEE AMENDMENT

COMMITTEE AMENDMENT “A” to H.P. 1491, L.D. 2212
1 4. Electing pass-through entity. "Electing pass-through entity" means a pass-through
2 entity that has elected for the taxable year to be subject to the tax imposed by this
3 subchapter.
4 5. Estimated tax. "Estimated tax" means the total amount of tax that an electing pass-
5 through entity estimates will be due for a taxable year under this subchapter, excluding the
6 estimated tax liability under section 5199-D, less any allowable credits for that taxable
7 year.
8 6. Pass-through entity. "Pass-through entity" means:
9 A. A partnership as defined in the Code, Section 7701(a)(2), excluding a publicly
10 traded partnership as defined in the Code, Section 7704(b); and
11 B. An entity classified as an S corporation under the Code for federal income tax
12 purposes.
13 "Pass-through entity" does not include an entity subject to tax under chapter 819.
14 7. Pass-through entity taxable income. "Pass-through entity taxable income" means
15 the aggregate total of distributive share of income of all qualified members for the taxable
16 year increased by the amount of the tax imposed on the pass-through entity by this
17 subchapter and by the amount of the tax imposed on the pass-through entity by another
18 state of the United States, a political subdivision of any such state or the District of
19 Columbia that is substantially the same as the tax imposed under this subchapter.
20 8. Period of underpayment. "Period of underpayment" means the period of time
21 from the date an estimated tax installment under section 5199-H is due until the
22 underpayment is satisfied or until the tax return to which the estimated tax installment
23 applies is due, whichever is less.
24 9. Qualified member. "Qualified member" means an individual, trust or estate that
25 is a direct owner, partner or shareholder in an electing pass-through entity, except that
26 "qualified member" does not include an organization exempt from tax under the Code,
27 Section 501. For purposes of this subsection, "direct owner" includes a qualified member
28 whose ownership in the electing pass-through entity is held through an entity that is
29 disregarded under the Code for federal income tax purposes.
30 §5199-B. Election of pass-through entity tax
31 A pass-through entity that elects to be subject to the tax imposed by this subchapter
32 shall declare that election annually on a timely filed return, including any extension granted
33 for filing the return. The election is irrevocable after the due date, including any extension
34 granted, for filing the return.
35 §5199-C. Imposition of tax
36 Notwithstanding any provision of law to the contrary, for every taxable year beginning
37 on or after January 1, 2026, a tax is imposed on every electing pass-through entity. For
38 each taxable year, the tax is equal to the pass-through entity taxable income multiplied by
39 the highest marginal tax rate under section 5111 except for the income tax surcharge
40 imposed pursuant to section 5111, subsection 7. The tax imposed by this section may not
41 be reduced for a pass-through entity by any of the credits in chapter 822. Notwithstanding
42 any provision of law to the contrary, for all budgetary accounting purposes, receipts from
Page 150 - 132LR3049(02)
COMMITTEE AMENDMENT

COMMITTEE AMENDMENT “A” to H.P. 1491, L.D. 2212
413 the tax imposed by this section are individual income tax revenue and must be treated for
424 such purposes in the same manner as receipts from the tax imposed by chapter 803.
3 §5199-D. Estimated tax payment of 10% on behalf of nonresident qualified members
4 For every taxable year beginning on or after January 1, 2026, a pass-through entity
5 subject to the tax under this subchapter shall pay, in addition to the tax under section
6 5199-C, an estimated tax on behalf of its nonresident qualified members in an amount equal
7 to 10% of the amount of the tax required to be reported to each nonresident qualified
8 member for the taxable year by the electing pass-through entity in accordance with section
9 5199-G. The electing pass-through entity, no later than 30 days following the date the
10 Maine return of the electing pass-through entity is due in accordance with section 5199-E,
11 without regard to any extension, shall report to each nonresident qualified member the
12 amount of the estimated tax paid by the pass-through entity for the taxable year on behalf
13 of the nonresident qualified member. Notwithstanding any provision of law to the contrary,
14 for all budgetary accounting purposes, receipts from the tax imposed by this section are
15 individual income tax revenue and must be treated for such purposes in the same manner
16 as receipts from the tax imposed by chapter 803.
17 §5199-E. Filing of returns
18 1. Filing of returns. An electing pass-through entity shall file a return with respect to
19 the tax imposed by this subchapter on such forms as may be required by the assessor. The
20 return must contain such information as required by the assessor, including the name and
21 social security or federal identification number of each qualified member and, for the
22 taxable year, each qualified member's distributive share of income, distributive share of tax
23 paid by the electing pass-through entity pursuant to this subchapter, the amount of the
24 estimated tax paid by the entity under section 5199-D and the distributive share of tax paid
25 to another state of the United States, a political subdivision of any such state or the District
26 of Columbia that is substantially the same as the tax imposed by this subchapter. The
27 distributive share of the tax paid to another state of the United States, a political subdivision
28 of any such state or the District of Columbia that is substantially the same as the tax
29 imposed by this subchapter with respect to qualified members that are not residents of the
30 State may be reported on an aggregated basis. The return required by this subsection must
31 be filed, along with any tax due, on or before the 15th day of the 3rd month following the
32 end of the electing pass-through entity's taxable year for federal income tax purposes. The
33 return, including related schedules and worksheets, required by this subsection must be
34 filed with the bureau by electronic data submission.
35 2. Consent required to amend returns. Notwithstanding the requirement to file an
36 amended return pursuant to section 5227-A, an electing pass-through entity may not amend
37 a return filed under this subchapter without the written consent of, or as otherwise
38 authorized by, the assessor.
39 §5199-F. Nonresident qualified member filing exception
40 1. Nonresident individual. A qualified member of an electing pass-through entity
41 who is a nonresident individual for the entire taxable year is not required to file an income
42 tax return pursuant to section 5220, subsection 2 for the same taxable year if:
Page 151 - 132LR3049(02)
COMMITTEE AMENDMENT

COMMITTEE AMENDMENT “A” to H.P. 1491, L.D. 2212
1 A. The member's entire Maine adjusted gross income, including a spouse's income in
2 the case of married individuals filing a joint federal return, is from one or more electing
3 pass-through entities;
4 B. The electing pass-through entity or entities file and pay the tax due under this
5 subchapter; and
6 C. The qualified member's credit provided by section 5219-CCC plus the estimated
7 tax amount paid by the electing pass-through entity on behalf of the nonresident
8 qualified member in accordance with section 5199-D is sufficient to satisfy the Maine
9 individual income tax liability of the member and the member's spouse for that taxable
10 year.
11 2. Nonresident trust or estate. A qualified member of an electing pass-through entity
12 that is a nonresident trust or estate for the entire taxable year is not required to file an
13 income tax return pursuant to section 5220, subsection 4 for the same taxable year if:
14 A. The member has its entire Maine-sourced income, as determined pursuant to section
15 5142, for the taxable year from one or more electing pass-through entities;
16 B. The member would have been required to include income described in paragraph
17 A as Maine taxable income if it were to file an income tax return for that year;
18 C. The member has not distributed any of its Maine-sourced income, as determined
19 pursuant to section 5142, for the taxable year to its beneficiaries as distributable net
20 income;
21 D. The electing pass-through entity or entities file and pay the tax due under this
22 subchapter; and
23 E. The member's credit provided by section 5219-CCC plus the estimated tax amount
24 paid by the electing pass-through entity on behalf of the nonresident qualified member
25 in accordance with section 5199-D is sufficient to satisfy the Maine income tax liability
26 for that taxable year.
27 §5199-G. Reporting tax to members
28 For purposes of the credit under section 5219-CCC, the electing pass-through entity,
29 no later than 30 days following the date the Maine return of the electing pass-through entity
30 is due in accordance with section 5199-E, shall report to each qualified member that
31 member's distributive share of tax. Each member report must clearly identify the member's
32 name and federal employer identification number or social security number, the member's
33 ownership interest in the entity, whether the member was a resident or nonresident of the
34 State during the taxable year, each member's distributive share of income and the
35 distributive share of tax paid to another state of the United States, a political subdivision of
36 any such state or the District of Columbia that is substantially the same as the tax imposed
37 by this subchapter and any other information that may be required by the assessor.
38 §5199-H. Estimated tax installment payments
39 Estimated taxes are required with respect to the tax imposed under section 5199-C in
40 accordance with the provisions of this section.
Page 152 - 132LR3049(02)
COMMITTEE AMENDMENT

COMMITTEE AMENDMENT “A” to H.P. 1491, L.D. 2212
1 1. Requirement to pay estimated tax. Every person subject to taxation under this
2 subchapter shall make payment of estimated tax as required by this section. The
3 requirement to make estimated tax payments is waived if:
4 A. The electing pass-through entity's estimated tax liability is less than $1,000 for the
5 taxable year; or
6 B. The electing pass-through entity had less than $1,000 tax liability under this
7 subchapter, excluding the estimated tax liability under section 5199-D, for the
8 preceding taxable year.
9 2. Amount of estimated tax to be paid. Every electing pass-through entity required
10 to make payment of estimated tax under this subchapter is liable for an estimated tax that
11 is no less than the smaller of:
12 A. An amount equal to the electing pass-through entity's tax liability under this
13 subchapter, excluding the estimated tax liability under section 5199-D, for the
14 preceding taxable year, if that preceding year was a taxable year of 12 months; and
15 B. An amount equal to 90% of the electing pass-through entity's tax liability under this
16 subchapter, excluding the estimated tax liability under section 5199-D, for the current
17 taxable year.
18 3. Due dates for estimated tax installments. An installment payment is due the 15th
19 day of the 4th, 6th, 9th and 13th month following the beginning of the electing pass-through
20 entity's taxable year.
21 4. Amount of installment. The amount of estimated tax to be paid in a taxable year
22 by an electing pass-through entity must be paid in installments by the dates established in
23 subsection 3. The amount of the estimated tax must be paid in 4 equal installments unless
24 the taxpayer establishes by adequate record the actual distribution of tax liability and
25 allowable credits, or both, in which case the amount of the installment payments must be
26 adjusted accordingly and be determined in accordance with the portion of the electing pass-
27 through entity's estimated tax liability applicable to that portion of the electing pass-through
28 entity's taxable year completed by the close of the month preceding the installment's due
29 date less estimated tax payments already made for the taxable year.
30 5. Penalty. A penalty accrues automatically on underpayments of the required
31 installment amount for the period of underpayment at the rate provided pursuant to section
32 186. For cause, the assessor may waive or abate all or part of the penalty.
33 6. Short taxable year. For a taxable year of less than 12 months, the estimated tax
34 must be paid in full by the 15th day of the last month of the taxable year. For payment dates
35 falling within the short taxable year, payment must be made as provided in subsection 3.
36 7. Installments paid in advance. At the election of the electing pass-through entity,
37 an installment of estimated tax may be paid prior to the date prescribed for its payment.
38 §5199-I. Interest on refunds prohibited
39 Notwithstanding any provision of this Title to the contrary, interest may not be paid by
40 the assessor on an overpayment of the tax imposed by this subchapter.

1 §5217-A. Income tax paid to other taxing jurisdiction
2 A resident individual is allowed a credit against the tax otherwise due under this Part,
3 excluding the tax imposed by section 5203-C, equal to the amount determined in subsection
4 1 plus the amount determined in subsection 2, as limited by subsection 4.
5 1. Individual income tax. For individual income tax, the credit is the amount of
6 income tax imposed on that individual for the taxable year by another state of the United
7 States, a political subdivision of any such state, the District of Columbia or any political
8 subdivision of a foreign country that is analogous to a state of the United States with respect
9 to income subject to tax under this Part that is derived from sources in that taxing
10 jurisdiction.
11 2. Distributive share. If another state of the United States, a political subdivision of
12 any such state, the District of Columbia or any political subdivision of a foreign country
13 that is analogous to a state of the United States imposes an income tax on an individual,
14 trust or estate that is substantially similar to the income tax imposed on such an individual,
15 trust or estate under this Part, the credit is equal to the amount of the individual's distributive
16 share of tax imposed on a pass-through entity that is substantially similar to the tax imposed
17 under chapter 815, subchapter 3, reduced by any refundable credits to the extent the
18 refundable credits reduce the income tax imposed on the individual, trust or estate below
19 zero, with respect to income subject to tax under this Part that is derived from sources in
20 that taxing jurisdiction.
21 3. Sourcing of income. In determining whether income is derived from sources in
22 another jurisdiction described in subsections 1 and 2, the assessor may not employ the law
23 of the other jurisdiction but shall instead assume that a statute equivalent to section 5142
24 applies in that jurisdiction.
25 4. Credit limitation. The credit, for any of the specified taxing jurisdictions described
26 in subsections 1 and 2, may not exceed the proportion of the tax otherwise due under this
27 Part, excluding the tax imposed by section 5203-C, that the amount of the taxpayer's Maine
28 adjusted gross income derived from sources in that taxing jurisdiction bears to the
29 taxpayer's entire Maine adjusted gross income; except that, when a credit is claimed for
30 taxes paid to both a state and a political subdivision of a state, the total credit allowable for
31 those taxes does not exceed the proportion of the tax otherwise due under this Part,
32 excluding the tax imposed by section 5203-C, that the amount of the taxpayer's Maine
33 adjusted gross income derived from sources in the other state bears to the taxpayer's entire
34 Maine adjusted gross income.

1 Sec. T-1. Affordability Payment Program established; purpose. The State
2 has determined that residents face increasingly high costs for basic necessities of life and,
3 further, that the burden of these high costs warrants assistance from the State. The
4 Affordability Payment Program, referred to in this section as "the program," is established
5 within the Department of Administrative and Financial Services to promote the general
6 welfare by returning a portion of consumption taxes through a payment to individuals
7 disproportionately affected by those costs. The affordability payment is intended to offset
8 expenses that have been incurred, and will be incurred in fiscal year 2026-27, by those
9 individuals.
10 1. Definitions. As used in this section, unless the context otherwise indicates, the
11 following terms have the following meanings.
12 A. "Affordability payment" means a payment in the amount of $300 made pursuant to
13 the program.
14 B. "Assessor" means the State Tax Assessor.
15 C. "Eligible recipient" means an individual who:
16 (1) Filed, by October 15, 2026, a Maine income tax return as a full-year resident
17 of the State for the tax year;
18 (2) Had, and reported to the Department of Administrative and Financial Services,
19 Maine Revenue Services by October 15, 2026 on an income tax return, federal
20 adjusted gross income for the tax year of less than:
21 (a) For individuals filing married joint returns or surviving spouses permitted
22 to file a joint return, $100,000;
23 (b) For an individual filing as a head of household, $75,000;
24 (c) For a single individual, $50,000; or
25 (d) For a married individual filing a separate return, $50,000; and
26 (3) May not be claimed as a dependent on another taxpayer's return for the tax
27 year.
28 D. "Fund" means the Affordability Payment Program Fund Other Special Revenue
29 Funds account established by the State Controller pursuant to subsection 2.
30 E. "Tax year" means a tax year beginning on or after January 1, 2025 but not later than
31 December 31, 2025.
32 Any other terms used in this section have the same meanings as when used in a comparable
33 context in the Maine Revised Statutes, Title 36, Part 8 relating to Maine income taxes,
34 unless different meanings are clearly required.
35 2. Affordability Payment Program Fund Other Special Revenue Funds account
36 established. The State Controller shall establish within the Department of Administrative
37 and Financial Services the nonlapsing Affordability Payment Program Fund Other Special
38 Revenue Funds account, which is funded through a transfer from the available balance of
39 the Maine Budget Stabilization Fund pursuant to section 2.
40 3. Affordability payment. The assessor shall make affordability payments in
41 accordance with this subsection.
42 A. The assessor shall identify all eligible recipients.
Page 157 - 132LR3049(02)
COMMITTEE AMENDMENT

COMMITTEE AMENDMENT “A” to H.P. 1491, L.D. 2212
1 B. Beginning as soon as administratively feasible but not later than July 31, 2026, the
2 assessor shall make an affordability payment to each eligible recipient for Maine
3 income tax returns filed by July 1, 2026 for the tax year. Funds for the affordability
4 payments must come from the fund and, notwithstanding any provision of law to the
5 contrary, are not subject to setoff to debts owed to agencies of the State.
6 C. By December 31, 2026, the assessor shall make an affordability payment to each
7 eligible recipient who did not receive an affordability payment pursuant to paragraph
8 B. Funds for the affordability payments must come from the fund and, notwithstanding
9 any provision of law to the contrary, are not subject to setoff to debts owed to agencies
10 of the State.
11 D. An individual who has not received an affordability payment under paragraph B or
12 C may provide documentation to the assessor by March 1, 2027 showing that the
13 individual is an eligible recipient. The assessor shall review the documentation,
14 determine if the individual is an eligible recipient and notify the individual of any
15 adverse determination. This determination constitutes final agency action and is not
16 reviewable pursuant to the Maine Revised Statutes, Title 36, section 151.
17 E. By April 30, 2027, the assessor shall make an affordability payment to each eligible
18 recipient determined eligible pursuant to paragraph D. Funds for the affordability
19 payments must come from the fund and, notwithstanding any provision of law to the
20 contrary, are not subject to setoff to debts owed to agencies of the State.
21 4. State income tax subtraction modification. For tax years beginning on or after
22 January 1, 2026 but not later than December 31, 2027, in determining the taxable income
23 of an individual, within the meaning of the Maine Revised Statutes, Title 36, section 5122,
24 federal adjusted gross income must be reduced by an amount equal to the affordability
25 payment received by the individual, pursuant to this Part, to the extent the payment is
26 included in federal adjusted gross income for the taxable year. The assessor shall request
27 a ruling from the United States Internal Revenue Service that the affordability payments
28 are not subject to federal taxation.
29 5. Designation as unclaimed property. For purposes of the program, affordability
30 payment checks that remain undeposited on December 31, 2027 are to be treated as
31 unclaimed property but are not subject to the notice and receipt provisions established in
32 the Maine Revised Statutes, Title 33, section 2101 or the one-year dormancy period
33 specified in Title 33, section 2061 that would be applicable to such checks. The Treasurer
34 of State shall use unclaimed property systems and networks to find the proper recipients of
35 such checks as quickly as possible.
36 6. Application to municipal general assistance program. For purposes of the
37 municipal general assistance program established pursuant to the Maine Revised Statutes,
38 Title 22, chapter 1161, affordability payments are not income, as defined in Title 22, section
39 4301, subsection 7; may not be considered to reduce need, as defined in Title 22, section
40 4301, subsection 10; and may not be considered a potential resource, as described in Title
41 22, section 4317.
42 7. Taxability of affordability payment. The State finds that the great majority of
43 eligible recipients have paid at least $300 in Maine sales and excise taxes, which have
44 contributed to the balance in the Maine Budget Stabilization Fund. The State further finds
45 that the affordability payments authorized by this section in effect return a portion of those
Page 158 - 132LR3049(02)
COMMITTEE AMENDMENT

COMMITTEE AMENDMENT “A” to H.P. 1491, L.D. 2212
416 tax payments to individuals considered to be disproportionately affected by such taxes.
427 Accordingly, the State finds and intends that the affordability payments constitute a refund
438 of those taxes paid for the purpose of determining whether the payments are income subject
449 to tax under the United States Internal Revenue Code of 1986 and the State's conformity to
550 the United States Internal Revenue Code of 1986.

34 §220-A. Farmers Drought Relief Grant Resilience Program
35 1. Grant program established. The Farmers Drought Relief Grant Resilience

1 2. Eligibility. A farmer in the State may apply for a grant under the program if the
2 farmer needs to establish a source for irrigation water to alleviate the risk of crop losses
3 due to drought. The source for irrigation water must be sustainable, environmentally sound
4 and affordable.
5 3. Farmers Drought Relief Grant Resilience Program Fund. The Farmers Drought
6 Relief Grant Resilience Program Fund is established as a nonlapsing fund to provide
7 funding to achieve the purposes of the program. The department may use funds for
8 personnel services and to contract for professional services to carry out the purposes of this
9 section. The fund consists of any funds received from private and public sources. The
10 fund must be held separate and apart from all other money, funds and accounts. Any
11 balance remaining in the fund at the end of any fiscal year must be carried forward to the
12 next fiscal year.

8 §1912. Funding for Maine Title X grantees; substitute funding
9 For any fiscal year after state fiscal year 2024-25, if the funding previously received
10 by a Maine Title X grantee under the Title X program is reduced relative to the funds
11 received in fiscal year 2024-25 or is eliminated as a result of action by the Federal
12 Government or of a decision by the grantee to withdraw from receiving funding under the
13 Title X program due to the imposition of conditions attached to the funding, the department
14 shall grant state funds to the Maine Title X grantee equal to the difference between the
15 amount the grantee received from the Title X program in fiscal year 2024-25 and the
16 amount the grantee receives and accepts in any subsequent fiscal year.

1 B. That, pursuant to any federal law, the department may not pay using federal funds

4 2. State funding for services. Family planning services, as defined by section 1902,
5 subsection 4, and any other Medicaid-covered items or services lawfully provided to

7 funds may not be used to pay that provider, including any services provided between July

40 means a public preschool program offered by a public school in the State in partnership
41 with a community provider that is not located on the site of a public school and includes,
42 but is not limited to, a public preschool program offered at a private child care facility.

1 2. Eligibility. After consulting with the Department of Health and Human Services,

3 standards and requirements for participation in the grant program. Eligibility standards and
4 requirements must align with rules adopted by the Department of Health and Human
5 Services for the licensing of child care facilities, including, but not limited to, child care
6 centers, nursery schools and small child care facilities. Eligibility standards and
7 requirements must include, but are not limited to:

28 The organization shall submit an annual report to the joint standing committees of the

36 Sec. FF-3. Maine Health Data Organization and Department of Health and
37 Human Services to develop care gap plan. The Maine Health Data Organization,
38 referred to in this section as "the organization," and the Department of Health and Human
39 Services, referred to in this section as "the department," shall develop a plan to measure the
40 State's care gap. For the purposes of this section, "care gap" means the difference between
41 the home and community-based services individuals are authorized to receive and the
Page 173 - 132LR3049(02)
COMMITTEE AMENDMENT

COMMITTEE AMENDMENT “A” to H.P. 1491, L.D. 2212
412 services that they actually received. The organization and the department shall do the
423 following:
3 1. Work with the department's appropriate vendors to create a supplemental file to
4 report on the care gap for services provided under the sections of MaineCare policy
5 provided under rule Chapter 101: MaineCare Benefits Manual, Chapter II, Sections 12, 17,
6 18, 19, 20, 21, 28, 29, 40, 65, 92 and 96. This file must include the authorization data
7 elements needed for the organization to link this data with the claims and eligibility data
8 that the vendor currently submits to the organization on behalf of the Office of MaineCare
9 Services within the department. The organization must have access to the authorization,
10 claims and eligibility data necessary to calculate the care gap. Using the existing data
11 governance framework, the organization shall securely integrate multiple data streams,
12 manage and store the data and develop dashboards to display key data points over time;
13 2. Work with the department's appropriate vendors to create a uniform reporting system
14 for submitting key data elements to report on the care gap for services provided under the
15 department's Office of Aging and Disability Services, Part 1 rule Chapter 5, Office of
16 Aging and Disability Services Policy Manual, Sections 61, 63, 68 and 69. These data
17 elements that may need modification to allow for data collection include, but are not limited
18 to, the medical eligibility determination form, authorized care plan, invoiced payment data
19 and utilization reports; and
20 3. Determine if there is any data available to estimate the care gap for services provided
21 under the sections of MaineCare policy provided under rule Chapter 101: MaineCare
22 Benefits Manual, Chapter II, Sections 23 and 26 and those services provided under Section
23 65 that do not require prior authorization. If data is available, the organization and the
24 department shall estimate the care gap.
25 Following the modifications to contracts required in subsections 1 and 2, the
26 organization shall integrate multiple data streams, manage and store the data and develop
27 dashboards to display key data points over time. The organization may integrate any
28 available data for those MaineCare services that do not require prior authorization
29 described in subsection 3. In accordance with the Maine Revised Statutes, Title 22, section
30 8712-A, the organization shall submit a report to the joint standing committees of the
31 Legislature having jurisdiction over health and human services matters and over
32 appropriations and financial affairs no later than November 15, 2027. This report must
33 contain data on the care gap with a breakdown by MaineCare Benefits Manual sections and
34 Office of Aging and Disability Services Policy Manual sections, including any changes in
35 trends. The report must include information on progress toward developing the care gap
36 analysis, including any options for MaineCare services without prior authorization. The
37 joint standing committee of the Legislature having jurisdiction over health and human
38 services matters is authorized to report out legislation in response to the report in 2028. As
39 part of any proposed legislation, the joint standing committee of the Legislature having
40 jurisdiction over health and human services matters must consider the inclusion of the care
41 gap data and the full cost of providing the services identified in the care gap in one of the
42 existing statutorily required reports submitted by the department to the joint standing
43 committee of the Legislature having jurisdiction over health and human services matters.

25 PART WW
26 Sec. WW-1. 22 MRSA §1708-A is enacted to read:
27 §1708-A. Reimbursement to Maine Veterans' Homes
28 Nursing facility services provided by a veterans' facility must be reimbursed by the
29 MaineCare program at rates established by the department pursuant to this section. This
30 section applies to all funds, including federal funds, paid by an agency of the State to a
31 veterans' facility for nursing facility care.
32 1. Definitions. As used in this section, unless the context otherwise indicates, the
33 following terms have the following meanings.
34 A. "Nursing home" or "nursing facility" has the same meaning as in section 1812-A.
35 B. "Residential care facility" has the same meaning as in section 7852, subsection 14.
36 C. "Veterans' facility" means a nursing home that is operated by the Maine Veterans'
37 Homes at any location required under Title 37-B, section 601, subsection 2.
38 2. Per resident basis. Veterans' facilities must be reimbursed on a per resident daily
39 rate basis for services to residents covered under the MaineCare program.
Page 181 - 132LR3049(02)
COMMITTEE AMENDMENT

COMMITTEE AMENDMENT “A” to H.P. 1491, L.D. 2212
1 3. Rate calculations. Rate calculations under this section must follow the process and
2 principles set forth in section 3173-J to determine a daily rate that is reasonable and
3 adequate to cover the MaineCare allowable costs of operating veterans' facilities and to
4 reflect any meaningful cost differences between veterans' nursing home and nonveterans'
5 nursing home costs for the direct care and routine care cost components of the daily rate.
6 Adjustments to rates and reimbursement for purposes that include, but are not limited to,
7 audit procedures, quality and case mix must be made in the same way as those adjustments
8 are made for all other nursing facilities as set forth in department rule.
9 4. Annual adjustments for inflation. The daily rate amounts calculated under this
10 section must be annually adjusted for inflation to the same extent that MaineCare
11 reimbursement rates for nonveterans' facilities that are nursing homes and residential care
12 facilities are adjusted.
13 Sec. WW-2. Supplemental payments to Maine Veterans' Homes
14 residential care facilities in fiscal year 2026-27. The Department of Health and
15 Human Services shall make a one-time payment to the Maine Veterans' Homes residential
16 care facilities for the difference between MaineCare payments and actual allowed
17 MaineCare costs, as reported on the most recently filed cost reports. The department may
18 make these residential care facility payments in fiscal year 2026-27 to enable the
19 department to seek and receive approval for a state plan amendment from the United States
20 Department of Health and Human Services, Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services
21 with federal matching payments to the extent possible.
22 Sec. WW-3. Supplemental payments previously appropriated. Upon
23 implementation of the rate calculations process established in the Maine Revised Statutes,
24 Title 22, section 1708-A, any supplemental funding allocated or appropriated for the Maine
25 Veterans' Homes in Public Law 2021, chapter 528 that has not been expended may not be
26 used as supplemental payments but may be used for the purposes of this Part.

27 Sec. FFF-1. 12 MRSA §10108, sub-§13 is enacted to read:
28 13. Inland Fisheries Conservation and Enhancement Program. The Inland
29 Fisheries Conservation and Enhancement Program, referred to in this subsection as "the
30 program," is established within the department's fisheries and hatcheries division within
31 the Bureau of Resource Management.
32 A. The program must:
33 (1) Prioritize native inland fish conservation and the research, management and
34 enhancement of the State's native inland fish populations, including but not limited
35 to those identified by the department as species of greatest conservation need,
36 brook trout, lake whitefish, arctic charr and lake trout;
37 (2) Identify and implement actions to enhance native inland fish production and
38 stocking, including assessment of native inland fish production programs; and
39 (3) Work to conserve, protect and enhance the State's sport fish fisheries.
40 B. To the extent that resources allow, the program must support and incorporate the
41 following elements:
Page 184 - 132LR3049(02)
COMMITTEE AMENDMENT

COMMITTEE AMENDMENT “A” to H.P. 1491, L.D. 2212
1 (1) The design and implementation of regional and statewide fishery research
2 programs designed to conserve the State's native inland and sport fish fisheries and
3 promote the long-term sustainability and health of those fisheries;
4 (2) Investment in new technology, equipment and training programs to enhance
5 the success and effectiveness of the program;
6 (3) Effective communication regarding ongoing program actions, to promote
7 program success and collaboration;
8 (4) The establishment of collaborative partnerships to engage organizations,
9 communities and companies representing conservation groups, anglers, land trusts
10 and other organizations involved in outdoor recreation in implementing and
11 publicizing the program; and
12 (5) Engagement with representatives of various agencies, anglers and nonprofit
13 organizations to enhance and restore the habitats and populations of native inland
14 fisheries.
15 C. The commissioner may accept money, goods or services donated to the department
16 for the program. Money accepted by the commissioner under this paragraph must be
17 deposited into the fisheries and hatcheries division dedicated account.
18 D. The commissioner, or the commissioner's designee, shall report no later than
19 February 1st annually to the joint standing committee of the Legislature having
20 jurisdiction over inland fisheries and wildlife matters regarding program activities. The
21 report must describe the work being done to conserve, research, manage and enhance
22 the State's native inland fish populations and must include a summary of program
23 expenditures, including how those expenditures were allocated.
24 PART GGG

27 place:
28 §10252. Fish hatchery maintenance fund
29 1. Fund established. The fish hatchery maintenance fund, referred to in this section
30 as "the fund," is established in the department as a nonlapsing fund to be used by the
31 commissioner to fund or assist in funding engineering designs, statewide facilities
32 assessments and maintenance, repair and capital improvements at fish hatcheries and
33 feeding stations owned by the State. The fund may be used for overtime personnel services
34 costs when overtime for fish stocking and other hatchery-related work is operationally
35 required. The fund may support the design, construction and operation of additional state
36 fish hatcheries that support the mission of the department, as approved by the Legislature.
37 The fund may not be used to fund the general operating costs of an existing fish hatchery.
38 2. Funding. Notwithstanding section 10801, subsection 4, $2 from each fishing
39 license sold must be deposited into the fund. Additionally, money deposited pursuant to
40 Title 29-A, section 456-C, subsection 3, paragraph A, subparagraph (1) must be credited to
41 the fund. The commissioner may accept and deposit into the fund any monetary gifts,
42 donations or other contributions from public or private sources and must use that money
43 for the purposes specified in subsection 1.

13 Sec. OOO-2. 25 MRSA §2915-B is enacted to read:
14 §2915-B. Sexual assault forensic examination kit tracking system
15 1. Definitions. As used in this section, unless the context otherwise indicates, the
16 following terms have the following meanings.
17 A. "Crime laboratory" means the Maine State Police Crime Laboratory located in
18 Augusta or another accredited laboratory that provides forensic services to law
19 enforcement agencies for the purpose of analyzing forensic examination kits.
20 B. "Department" means the Department of Public Safety.
21 C. "Forensic examination kit" or "kit" means a uniform standardized forensic
22 examination kit developed by the department pursuant to section 2915, subsection 1
23 for evidence collection by a licensed health care provider in alleged cases of sexual
24 assault.
25 D. "Law enforcement agency" means the Bureau of State Police, a county law
26 enforcement agency or a municipal law enforcement agency.
27 E. "Licensed health care provider" means:
28 (1) A hospital licensed under Title 22, chapter 405; or
29 (2) A health care practitioner who is licensed by this State to provide or otherwise
30 lawfully providing health care or a partnership or corporation made up of such
31 health care practitioners.
32 F. "Sexual assault" means any crime enumerated in Title 17-A, chapter 11.
33 G. "Tracking system" means the forensic examination kit tracking system established
34 by the department pursuant to subsection 2.
35 2. Forensic examination kit tracking system. The department shall operate and
36 maintain a tracking system for all completed forensic examination kits, regardless of
37 whether the alleged offense relating to the kit was reported to a law enforcement agency,
38 that is continuously accessible to approved users as determined by department rule,
39 including, at a minimum, licensed hospitals and licensed health care providers that perform
40 forensic examinations for victims of sexual assault.
41 A. The department shall operate and maintain the tracking system through the use of
42 department personnel and resources and may contract with public or private entities,
Page 189 - 132LR3049(02)
COMMITTEE AMENDMENT

COMMITTEE AMENDMENT “A” to H.P. 1491, L.D. 2212
413 including, but not limited to, software and technology providers, to accomplish the
424 department's duties related to the tracking system.
3 B. Approved users shall adhere to the following procedures when entering information
4 for each kit into the tracking system, which must, at a minimum, collect the following
5 information.
6 (1) A crime laboratory shall enter into the tracking system each new and unused
7 kit and record each kit's intended destination before the kit leaves the possession
8 of the crime laboratory.
9 (2) A licensed health care provider shall enter into the tracking system:
10 (a) The date the licensed health care provider receives each kit from a crime
11 laboratory;
12 (b) The date a particular kit is used for evidence collection, is discarded based
13 on damage to the kit or is used for training; and
14 (c) The date a completed kit is transferred to a law enforcement agency, the
15 name of the law enforcement agency to which the kit was transferred and, to
16 the extent known to the licensed health care provider, whether the victim of
17 sexual assault from whom the forensic evidence in the kit was collected has
18 reported the alleged offense to a law enforcement agency.
19 (3) A law enforcement agency shall enter into the tracking system:
20 (a) The date the law enforcement agency receives a completed kit, the location
21 where that kit is being stored and, to the extent known by the law enforcement
22 agency, whether the victim of sexual assault from whom the forensic evidence
23 in the kit was collected has reported the alleged offense to a law enforcement
24 agency;
25 (b) The date the law enforcement agency transfers a completed kit to a crime
26 laboratory and the name of the crime laboratory to which the kit was
27 transferred; and
28 (c) The date the law enforcement agency reports to a prosecuting attorney that
29 a forensic examination has been performed pursuant to Title 24, section 2986.
30 (4) A crime laboratory shall identify when it has conducted an analysis of a
31 completed kit and enter into the tracking system the date of the analysis.
32 (5) A law enforcement agency that receives a kit after analysis from a crime
33 laboratory shall enter into the tracking system the date it received the kit and
34 whether that kit was stored or destroyed.
35 C. The tracking system must provide for the updating and tracking of a forensic
36 examination kit as follows.
37 (1) A victim of sexual assault from whom forensic evidence has been collected
38 using a kit must have access to the tracking system on an anonymous and secure
39 basis for the purposes of receiving updates regarding the kit and tracking the status
40 of the kit.
41 (2) A licensed health care provider, law enforcement agency, prosecuting attorney
42 or any other entity in possession of a kit must have access to the tracking system
Page 190 - 132LR3049(02)
COMMITTEE AMENDMENT

COMMITTEE AMENDMENT “A” to H.P. 1491, L.D. 2212
413 for the purposes of entering, updating and tracking the status and location of the
424 kit.
3 (3) A prosecuting attorney in the State must have access to the tracking system for
4 the purpose of receiving updates regarding the kits and tracking the status of the
5 kits.
6 3. Participation required. Law enforcement agencies, licensed health care providers,
7 crime laboratories and prosecuting attorneys in the State shall participate in the tracking
8 system on the schedule and in the manner required by department rules adopted pursuant
9 to subsection 7.
10 4. Immunity. A person or entity required by subsection 3 to participate in the tracking
11 system is immune from civil liability for actions taken as a participant that are required by
12 subsection 3 or by rules adopted pursuant to subsection 7.
13 5. Confidentiality. The tracking system and all information in the tracking system is
14 confidential.
15 6. Report. The department shall report to the joint standing committee of the
16 Legislature having jurisdiction over criminal justice and public safety matters and the
17 Governor by January 1, 2028 and annually thereafter regarding the tracking system. The
18 report may include recommendations from the department for improved operation of the
19 tracking system and must include the following information, identified by the county from
20 which a forensic examination kit was entered into the tracking system:
21 A. The number of completed kits in the tracking system, including:
22 (1) The number of completed kits for which a victim of sexual assault has reported
23 the alleged offense to a law enforcement agency and, of those kits, the number on
24 which analysis has been completed and the number on which analysis has not been
25 completed; and
26 (2) The number of completed kits for which a victim of sexual assault has not
27 reported the alleged offense to a law enforcement agency and, of those kits, the
28 number on which analysis has been completed and the number on which analysis
29 has not been completed;
30 B. The number of completed kits that were entered into the tracking system during the
31 prior calendar year, including:
32 (1) The number of completed kits for which a victim of sexual assault has reported
33 the alleged offense to a law enforcement agency and, of those kits, the number on
34 which analysis has been completed and the number on which analysis has not been
35 completed; and
36 (2) The number of completed kits for which a victim of sexual assault has not
37 reported the alleged offense to a law enforcement agency and, of those kits, the
38 number on which analysis has been completed and the number on which analysis
39 has not been completed;
40 C. The average and median lengths of time for forensic examination kits to be sent for
41 analysis after being entered into the tracking system and for analysis to be completed;
42 and
Page 191 - 132LR3049(02)
COMMITTEE AMENDMENT

COMMITTEE AMENDMENT “A” to H.P. 1491, L.D. 2212
1 D. The number of reported forensic examination kits entered into the tracking system
2 for more than one year for which analysis has not been completed.
3 A law enforcement agency that performs its duties in more than one county shall provide
4 to the department tracking information for each county where a forensic examination kit
5 was entered into the tracking system.
6 7. Rules. The commissioner shall adopt rules to govern participation in and
7 implementation of the tracking system. The rules must include provisions for maintaining
8 confidentiality of tracking system information; provisions to ensure timely posting to the
9 tracking system; a process for approving tracking system users and access for approved
10 users; procedures for posting information and for updating information in the tracking
11 system; and requirements for documentation of persons who have posted, accessed and
12 updated information in the tracking system. Rules adopted pursuant to this subsection are
13 routine technical rules as defined by Title 5, chapter 375, subchapter 2-A.
14 Sec. OOO-3. 25 MRSA §3821, as amended by PL 2025, c. 109, §6, is further
15 amended to read:
16 §3821. Transportation and storage of forensic examination kits
17 For the purposes of this section, "sexual assault" means any crime enumerated in Title
18 17‑A, chapter 11 and "strangulation" has the same meaning as in Title 17‑A, section 208,
19 subsection 1, paragraph C.
20 If a victim of sexual assault or strangulation has a forensic examination and has not
21 reported the alleged offense to a law enforcement agency when the examination is
22 complete, the licensed hospital or licensed health care practitioner that completed the
23 forensic examination shall notify the nearest law enforcement agency. That law
24 enforcement agency shall transport the completed forensic examination kit, identified only
25 by a tracking number assigned by the kit manufacturer, to its evidence storage facility. The
26 law enforcement agency shall store the forensic examination kits involving sexual assault
27 for 20 years and forensic examination kits involving only strangulation for 6 years. If
28 during that storage period the victim reports the offense to a law enforcement agency, the
29 investigating agency shall take possession of the forensic examination kit.
30 If a victim of sexual assault or strangulation has a forensic examination and has
31 reported the alleged offense to a law enforcement agency when the forensic examination is
32 complete, or the victim reports the alleged offense after the forensic examination kit has
33 been transported to the nearest law enforcement agency, the law enforcement agency
34 investigating the alleged offense shall take possession of that kit.
35 A law enforcement agency shall store the forensic examination kits involving sexual
36 assault for 20 years and forensic examination kits involving only strangulation for 6 years,
37 regardless of whether the alleged offense relating to the kit is reported to a law enforcement
38 agency.
39 In the case of a forensic examination performed under Title 24, section 2986,
40 subsection 5, the law enforcement agency must immediately notify the district attorney for
41 the district in which the hospital or health care practitioner is located that such a forensic
42 examination has been performed and a forensic examination kit has been completed under
43 Title 24, section 2986, subsection 5.

1 Sec. OOO-4. Sexual assault forensic examination kit inventory. By October

6 of:

11 2. Completed kits for which a victim of sexual assault has reported the alleged offense
12 to a law enforcement agency and the kit has not been analyzed by a crime laboratory. For
13 purposes of this section, "crime laboratory" has the same meaning as in the Maine Revised
14 Statutes, Title 25, section 2915-B, subsection 1, paragraph A; and

21 §12723. Maine Free Community College Program
22 The Maine Free Community College Program, referred to in this section as "the
23 program," is established. The system shall waive tuition for no more than 2 years for an
24 eligible student who meets the conditions under subsection 2. The availability of the tuition
25 waiver is limited to the amount appropriated.
26 1. Definition. For purposes of this section, unless the context otherwise indicates,
27 "eligible student" means a person who graduated from high school in the previous 2
28 academic years or obtained the equivalent of a high school diploma in the previous 2
29 academic years.
30 2. Conditions. In order to receive a tuition waiver under this section, an eligible
31 student must:
32 A. Pursue an associate degree or other academic credential;
33 B. Have established a primary residence in the State for at least 12 consecutive months
34 immediately prior to the date of admission to a college and reside in the State for the
35 duration of the student's enrollment in the college;
36 C. Accept all available federal and state grants, scholarships and other sources of
37 funding; and
38 D. Access the tuition waiver for no longer than 150% of the time stated by a college
39 for completion of a program of study.
40 3. Report. No later than January 15th of each year, the system shall prepare and
41 submit a report regarding the program to the Governor and the joint standing committee of
Page 194 - 132LR3049(02)
COMMITTEE AMENDMENT

COMMITTEE AMENDMENT “A” to H.P. 1491, L.D. 2212
412 the Legislature having jurisdiction over education matters and the joint standing committee
423 of the Legislature having jurisdiction over appropriations and financial affairs.

26 §9011. Enforcement and penalties
27 1. Inspection of violations. The board office may, upon complaint or probable cause,
28 inspect the manufactured housing, manufacturing facilities, a licensee's business facilities
29 or such records as may be necessary to verify whether a violation has occurred. If the board
30 office finds that a violation has occurred, it shall proceed pursuant to section 9009.
31 2. Petition to initiate legal action. The board office may petition the Attorney
32 General to initiate legal action in any court of competent jurisdiction for monetary or
33 injunctive relief to enforce this chapter.
34 3. Penalties. Any A person found guilty of violation of this chapter may be required
35 to pay a civil penalty of not more than $1,000 for each such violation, but not more than
36 $5,000 for an action consisting of separate violations.
37 4. Private actions. The private rights of action created by this subsection are in
38 addition to any rights of enforcement and relief granted to the board office in this chapter.
39 The board office shall notify all claimants of their right to seek remedy.
40 A. A person damaged as a result of a violation of this chapter also has a cause of action
41 in court against the person responsible for the manufacture, brokering, distribution,
42 sale, lease, installation or service, and the court may award appropriate damages and
43 cost for litigation in its judgment.

1 A. The State or a political subdivision of the State may deny a certificate of occupancy
2 for any certified manufactured housing when, in the exercise of reasonable judgment,
3 the State or the political subdivision of the State determines that an imminent and direct
4 risk of serious physical injury or death would exist in the normal use of the
5 manufactured housing.
6 B. If a certificate of occupancy for certified manufactured housing is denied, the State
7 or a political subdivision of the State shall promptly provide the applicant for the
8 certificate of occupancy with written notice of the denial. The notice must describe
9 each reason for the denial of the certificate of occupancy in sufficient detail to allow
10 the applicant to correct each deficiency noted. The State or a political subdivision of
11 the State shall simultaneously provide a copy of the notice to the board office.
12 C. If the code enforcement or other inspection officer of the State or a political
13 subdivision of the State identifies a failure of certified manufactured housing to comply
14 with this chapter or any building code or rule adopted by the board office, the officer
15 may file a complaint with the board office in accordance with section 9051.
16 D. This chapter may not be construed to impose a duty on a code enforcement or other
17 inspection officer of the State or a political subdivision of the State to inspect any
18 certified manufactured housing for compliance with this chapter or any building code
19 or rule adopted by the board office. Unless a certificate of occupancy has been issued
20 pursuant to the provisions of section 9043, subsection 2, paragraph A, a certificate of
21 occupancy for certified manufactured housing does not constitute a representation by
22 the State or a political subdivision of the State that the manufactured housing meets the
23 standards established under this chapter.
24 6. Review of denial; issuance of certificate of occupancy. Notwithstanding the
25 provisions of Title 25, chapter 313, if a certificate of occupancy for certified manufactured
26 housing is denied on account of any alleged failure of the manufactured housing to comply
27 with this chapter or any building code or rule adopted by the board office or any law, rule,
28 regulation or ordinance from which the manufactured housing is exempt under this chapter,
29 the applicant for the certificate of occupancy may petition the board office to review the
30 denial.
31 The board office shall issue a certificate of occupancy for the manufactured housing if,
32 after appropriate notice and administrative hearing in accordance with Title 5, chapter 375,
33 subchapter IV 4, the board office determines that:
34 A. The manufactured housing has been certified pursuant to the rules adopted by the
35 board office;
36 B. The certificate of occupancy was not denied pursuant to subsection 5, paragraph A;
37 and
38 C. The notice of denial issued under subsection 5, paragraph B does not specify any
39 violation of applicable law, rule, regulation or ordinance from which the manufactured
40 housing is not exempted under this chapter.
41 A certificate of occupancy issued under this subsection has the same validity, force and
42 effect as if issued by the State or a political subdivision of the State in which the
43 manufactured housing is located.

3 §9043. Approval alternatives

17 Sec. RRR-58. Transition provisions; Manufactured Housing Board. The
18 following provisions govern the transition of the responsibilities of the Manufactured
19 Housing Board, and its supporting services provided by the Department of Professional
20 and Financial Regulation, Office of Professional and Occupational Regulation, to the
21 Maine Office of Community Affairs.
22 1. The Maine Office of Community Affairs is the successor in every way to the powers,
23 duties and functions of the Manufactured Housing Board, including acting as the state
24 administrative agency under the National Manufactured Housing Construction and Safety
25 Standards Act of 1974.
26 2. All existing rules, regulations and procedures in effect, in operation or adopted in
27 or by the Manufactured Housing Board or on its behalf by the Office of Professional and
28 Occupational Regulation or any of the board's administrative units or officers are declared
29 in effect and continue in effect until rescinded, revised or amended by the Maine Office of
30 Community Affairs.
31 3. All existing contracts, agreements and compacts currently in effect under the
32 authority of the Manufactured Housing Board or on its behalf by the Office of Professional
33 and Occupational Regulation or any of the board's administrative units or officers continue
34 in effect until supplanted by rules established by the Maine Office of Community Affairs.
35 4. All records, property and equipment previously belonging to or allocated for the use
36 of the Manufactured Housing Board become, on the effective date of this Part, part of the
37 property of the Maine Office of Community Affairs.
38 5. All existing forms, licenses, letterheads and similar items bearing the name of or
39 referring to the Manufactured Housing Board may be used by the Maine Office of
40 Community Affairs until existing supplies of those items are exhausted.
41 6. Employees of the Maine Office of Community Affairs who were employees of the

3 PART YYY
4 Sec. YYY-1. Supplemental payment to hospitals. The Department of Health
5 and Human Services shall determine a consistent and reasonable allocation method to
6 distribute a one-time supplemental payment to acute care hospitals. The amount of the
7 payment must equal the amount appropriated and allocated for this purpose by this Act but
8 may be proportionally reduced if revenues collected pursuant to the Maine Revised
9 Statutes, Title 36, chapter 377 are less than the allocation. Additionally, total payment
10 amounts may not exceed the lower of the amount appropriated for supplemental payments
11 by this Act and the amount by which any payment would cause the department to exceed
12 allowable aggregate upper payment limits as determined by the United States Department
13 of Health and Human Services, Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services. The
14 department may make these payments in fiscal year 2026-27 to enable the department to
15 seek and receive approval for a state plan amendment from the United States Department
16 of Health and Human Services, Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services with federal
17 matching payments to the extent possible.
18 Sec. YYY-2. Cost-of-living adjustment. Notwithstanding any provision of law
19 to the contrary, the Department of Health and Human Services shall submit the required
20 methodology notice necessary to seek approval from the United States Department of
21 Health and Human Services, Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services to implement a
22 cost-of-living adjustment for inpatient diagnosis-related group reimbursement rates subject
23 to rule Chapter 101: MaineCare Benefits Manual, Chapter II, Section 45 effective July 1,
24 2026. The amount of the cost-of-living adjustment must be determined based on amounts
25 appropriated and allocated for this purpose by this Act and actual revenues collected
26 pursuant to the Maine Revised Statutes, Title 36, chapter 377.
27 Sec. YYY-3. Supplemental payments and increases in rates contingent.
28 Any supplemental payments and increases to hospital inpatient reimbursement rates
29 provided for in this Part with funding made available due to changing the base year of the
30 hospital tax are contingent upon the Department of Health and Human Services seeking
31 and being approved for any applicable tax waivers from the United States Department of
32 Health and Human Services, Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services pursuant to 42
33 Code of Federal Regulations, Section 433.72.

29 PART GGGG
30 Sec. GGGG-1. 30-A MRSA c. 201, sub-c. 15 is enacted to read:
31 SUBCHAPTER 15
32 MAINE EVICTION PREVENTION PROGRAM
33 §4994-D. Maine Eviction Prevention Program
34 The Maine Eviction Prevention Program, referred to in this section as "the program,"
35 is established within the Maine State Housing Authority to provide rental assistance to
36 individuals.
37 1. Eligibility; income. The Maine State Housing Authority shall establish eligibility
38 criteria and application requirements for participation in the program consistent with the
39 requirements under this subchapter. A program applicant's income may not exceed 60%
40 of the area median income, adjusted for family size, in accordance with federal standards
Page 227 - 132LR3049(02)
COMMITTEE AMENDMENT

COMMITTEE AMENDMENT “A” to H.P. 1491, L.D. 2212
411 generally accepted as of the effective date of this subchapter and comparable to the
422 standards of the United States Department of Housing and Urban Development.
3 2. Rental assistance. A participant in the program is eligible to receive up to $800
4 per month in rental assistance paid directly to the participant's landlord for up to 12 months.
5 3. Assistance priority. Rental assistance under this subchapter must be awarded in
6 the following order of priority:
7 A. As the first priority, participants who have received a summons and complaint for
8 forcible entry and detainer;
9 B. As the 2nd priority, participants who receive an eviction notice for nonpayment of
10 rent; and
11 C. As the 3rd priority, participants who pay over 30% of their income in rent.
12 4. Payment requirement. A participant in the program is required to pay 30% of the
13 participant's income toward the participant's rental obligation.
14 5. Rent requirement. A participant in the program must reside in a rental unit in
15 which the rent is at or below 125% of the fair market rent established by the United States
16 Department of Housing and Urban Development pursuant to 24 Code of Federal
17 Regulations, Section 888.113 for the household size in the area in which the rental unit is
18 located.
19 6. Eviction for cause. A landlord may evict a program participant only for cause
20 during any month rental assistance is paid by the program.
21 7. Program administration. The Maine State Housing Authority shall delegate
22 administration of the program and provide appropriate funding to one or more of the
23 following: a designated community action agency as described by Title 22, section 5324;
24 a municipal housing authority; or any other qualified entity as determined by the Maine
25 State Housing Authority. An entity delegated to administer the program shall develop a
26 method of verifying a participant's eligibility for the rental assistance every 6 months after
27 initial approval. An entity delegated to administer the program shall limit administrative
28 costs of the program to 12% or less of distributions received.
29 8. Rules. The Maine State Housing Authority may adopt rules to implement the
30 provisions of this subchapter. Rules adopted pursuant to this subsection are routine
31 technical rules as defined in Title 5, chapter 375, subchapter 2-A.

9 SUMMARY
1100 PART A
11 This Part makes appropriations and allocations of funds for fiscal year 2026-27.
12 PART B
13 This Part makes appropriations and allocations of funds for approved reclassifications
14 and range changes.
15 PART C
16 This Part establishes the total cost of education from kindergarten to grade 12, the state
17 contribution, the annual target state share percentage and the mill expectation for the local
18 contribution for fiscal year 2026-27.
19 PART D
20 This Part authorizes limited-period positions funded with Federal Expenditure Funds -
21 ARP State Fiscal Recovery funds to be extended through May 31, 2027 and authorizes the
22 transfer of Federal Expenditures Fund - ARP State Fiscal Recovery funds within or
23 between departments or agencies during fiscal year 2026-27.
24 PART E
25 This Part repeals the provision of law that requires that the cost of administration for
26 the state employee health insurance program established in the Maine Revised Statutes,
27 Title 5, chapter 13, subchapter 2 be funded from an administrative allowance negotiated by
28 the State Employee Health Commission with the health benefit carrier. It also repeals the
29 provision of law establishing a separate dedicated revenue account for the administration
30 of the program, the State Employee Assistance Program and the workers' compensation
31 unit. This Part removes the cross-reference to this fund from the provision of law
32 establishing the State Employee Assistance Program and specifies that the Department of
33 Administrative and Financial Services must receive and disburse funds made available to
34 the State Employee Assistance Program through the state employee health insurance
35 program.
36 This Part also specifies that the reserve fund for the state employee health insurance
37 program established in the Maine Revised Statutes, Title 5, section 286 is created within
38 the Accident, Sickness and Health Insurance Internal Service Fund.
39 PART F
40 This Part changes from $400,000 to $4,000,000 the amount that the Governor, during
41 a declared state of emergency and in accordance with rules adopted by the Commissioner

434 kitchens, that provide or distribute food to low-income, indigent or unemployed individuals

18 PART H
19 This Part clarifies that interest earned on investments of money from internal service
20 funds must be credited to the General Fund, unless otherwise specified.
21 PART I
22 This Part requires state postal services to be available to all departments and agencies
23 of State Government and allows postal services to be available to the University of Maine
24 System and political subdivisions, educational institutions or qualifying nonprofit
25 organizations in the State. This Part also codifies the long-standing state purchasing card

27 Bureau of General Services, Central Services Division.

32 Financing Fund.

37 1986 as amended through December 31, 2025;
38 2. Phases in Maine conformity with the federal deduction for domestic research or
39 experimental expenditures over the period of 2026 to 2030, except that, for small
40 businesses that meet the federal gross receipts test, conformity applies fully;
41 3. Decouples Maine tax law from federal provisions that allow corporate taxpayers to
42 immediately claim an income tax deduction for certain qualified production property

413 constructed after January 19, 2025 and before January 1, 2029 and placed in service before
424 January 1, 2031 by creating an addition modification to reflect the additional federal

22 PART L
23 This Part amends the base year for the hospital tax from the hospital fiscal year ending
24 in calendar year 2022 to the hospital fiscal year ending in calendar year 2024. This change
25 applies retroactively to tax due for the state fiscal year beginning July 1, 2025.
26 PART M
27 This Part consolidates and reorganizes the property tax exemptions for homesteads,
28 veterans and persons who are legally blind into one tiered homestead exemption beginning
29 on or after April 1, 2027 as follows.
30 1. The base exemption amount is up to $25,000 of the just value of the homestead,
31 with additional exemption amounts for veterans and individuals who are legally blind.
32 2. The additional exemptions for veterans are based on age and disability ratings.
33 3. The additional exemption for veterans eliminates complex service period
34 requirements and age and disability requirements that exist in the current exemption for
35 veterans, with all veterans eligible for at least a partial exemption.
36 4. The existing property tax exemptions for veterans and individuals who are legally
37 blind are discontinued.
38 5. The administrative provisions for the homestead exemption are reorganized.
39 6. Municipal reimbursement is required for all veterans and individuals who are legally
40 blind at the existing homestead reimbursement rate of 76%.
41 7. It amends the abatement and the municipal veterans' assistance program to reflect
42 the new veterans' exemption language.
Page 231 - 132LR3049(02)

COMMITTEE AMENDMENT “A” to H.P. 1491, L.D. 2212
1 8. It removes a reference to the existing veterans' exemption and adds a definition of
2 "veteran" to the laws regarding the property tax fairness credit.
3 This Part also directs the State Tax Assessor to develop and make publicly available
4 guidance regarding the consolidated homestead exemption, including the transition and
5 continuation of eligibility provisions.
6 PART N
7 This Part enacts a pass-through entity tax for tax years beginning on or after January 1,
8 2026. The tax applies to each taxable year that the pass-through entity elects to be subject
9 to the tax. The tax is equal to the distributive share of income of all qualified members
10 multiplied by the highest marginal individual income tax rate, which is currently 7.15%,
11 but does not include the 2% income tax surcharge. Qualified members may claim a
12 refundable income tax credit equal to 90% of their distributive share of the pass-through
13 entity tax paid.
14 PART O
15 This Part phases out reimbursement for property located at a retail sales facility and
16 used primarily in a retail sales activity under the Business Equipment Tax Reimbursement
17 program. Reimbursement is 50% for taxes paid on retail property during calendar year
18 2027 and is not available for taxes paid on retail property during calendar year 2028 and
19 after.
20 PART P

1 PART Z
2 This Part does the following.
3 1. It establishes the Fund to Maintain Access to Statewide Family Planning Services to
4 provide a grant to a single qualified provider to provide management and oversight of the
5 delivery of family planning services. It is the intent of the Legislature that, beginning in
6 fiscal year 2027-28, $5,000,000 be appropriated from the General Fund to the fund each
7 year.
8 2. It requires the Department of Health and Human Services to provide funding to
9 family planning services providers in the State if funding under Title X of the federal Public
10 Health Service Act has been reduced or eliminated by the Federal Government or the
11 provider has withdrawn from Title X funding due to the imposition of conditions attached
12 to the funding.
13 3. It requires the Department of Health and Human Services to cover family planning
14 services for MaineCare members who receive services from a provider that is a prohibited
15 provider under Section 71113 of federal Public Law 119-21 (2025) and any future similar
16 federal law. Services provided after July 1, 2025 are reimbursed retroactively.
