6 PART FF
7 This Part changes the membership of the Essential Support Workforce Advisory
8 Committee to include a representative of the long-term care ombudsman program and a
9 representative of an organization that provides personal care services in the home.
10 This Part applies a one-time cost-of-living adjustment of 3.07% for direct care services
11 provided under certain sections of the MaineCare Benefits Manual and state-funded home
12 and community-based services. The adjustment is applied beginning January 1, 2027.
13 This Part requires the Maine Health Data Organization and the Department of Health
14 and Human Services to work with the department's vendors to allow for the organization
15 to develop a plan to measure the care gap in essential support worker services paid by the
16 MaineCare program or a similar state-funded program. "Care gap" is defined as the
17 difference between the services individuals are authorized to receive and the services that
18 they actually received. This follows the recommendations from the report submitted
19 pursuant to Resolve 2025, chapter 113. This Part also requires the joint standing committee
20 of the Legislature having jurisdiction over health and human services matters to determine
21 which existing reports required by statute that are submitted by the department to the
22 committee could be amended to include the ongoing care gap funding requirements.
23 PART GG
24 This Part requires a school board to adopt and implement a policy prohibiting student
25 use of personal electronic devices, including cellular telephones, for the duration of the
26 school day. This Part also directs the Department of Education, in coordination with the
27 Maine School Management Association and other relevant stakeholders, to make available
28 a model policy for school boards regarding this prohibition.
29 PART HH
30 This Part repeals the Maine Revised Statutes, Title 20-A, chapter 114-A, which
31 establishes and governs the Fund for the Efficient Delivery of Educational Services. This
32 Part also repeals provisions that reference that chapter or that fund.
33 PART II
34 This Part increases the maximum age allowed for enrollment in a public elementary or
35 secondary school from 19 years of age at the start of a school year to 21 years of age at the
36 start of a school year.
37 PART JJ
38 This Part includes as an allowable use of the Preschool Special Education Program
39 Fund paying costs attributed to professional and administrative staff support to implement
40 the provision of special education services to children eligible under Part B, Section 619 of
41 the federal Individuals with Disabilities Education Act, 20 United States Code, Section
42 1400 et seq. It also clarifies that a school administrative unit’s unexpended funds from an
Page 235 - 132LR3049(02)
COMMITTEE AMENDMENT

COMMITTEE AMENDMENT “A” to H.P. 1491, L.D. 2212
413 allocation of this fund at the end of a fiscal year must be carried forward to reduce the net
424 preschool expenses in the next fiscal year.
3 PART KK
4 This Part moves the funding to support students 16 years of age or older and under 20
5 years of age who are no longer enrolled in public education from the essential programs
6 and services funding calculation to the adult education program where these students are
7 taking courses to attain high school equivalency diplomas. This change will better align
8 with current statute, improve accuracy, simplify reporting, provide better equity and
9 increase the impact of funding intended for those students no longer enrolled in public
10 education.
11 PART LL
12 This Part adds an alternate data set to determine teacher eligibility for a national board
13 certification salary supplement. The alternate data set is based on the percentage of
14 students identified as economically disadvantaged for subsidy purposes under the Maine
15 Revised Statutes, Title 20-A, chapter 606-B.
16 PART MM
17 This Part moves principal and interest costs for approved major capital projects from
18 payment within the essential programs and services funding calculation to payment as
19 targeted education funds. This allows the essential programs and services funding
20 calculation for each school administrative unit to be determined prior to inclusion of debt
21 service costs and attributes debt service costs only to those school administrative units with
22 approved major capital projects.
23 PART NN
24 This Part repeals the provision of law that authorizes the Commissioner of Education
25 to expend and disburse funds from the Fund for the Efficient Delivery of Educational
26 Services.
27 PART OO
28 This Part requires that any remaining balances of appropriations in the Department of
29 Education, Special Services Team program carry forward each fiscal year for the same
30 purpose.
31 PART PP
32 This Part directs the transfer of $16,651,457 in unencumbered balance forward from
33 the General Purpose Aid for Local Schools program to the Child Development Services
34 program within the Department of Education.
35 PART QQ
36 This Part authorizes the Department of Education to transfer Personal Services
37 appropriations to the All Other line category within 3 programs in order to fund
38 maintenance and licensing costs for an Internet-based application for free or reduced-price
39 meals, annual hosting and maintenance costs for the department’s grant management
40 system and rising costs related to the adult education data system.

4 PART SS
5 This Part authorizes the Department of Environmental Protection to transfer available
6 balances of Personal Services appropriations to the Air Quality program, General Fund
7 account, Capital Expenditures line category in order to fund the purchase of 2 ozone
8 calibrator instruments, one ozone monitor instrument, one continuous, real-time air quality
9 monitoring instrument, one zero air generator, 2 hydrogen sulfide analyzers, 2 trace-level
10 dilution calibrators and 2 data loggers.
11 PART TT
12 This Part allows the Finance Authority of Maine to issue revenue obligation securities
13 for energy distribution system projects or energy generating system projects that have been
14 issued a certificate of approval by the authority before January 1, 2040, as opposed to
15 current law, which prohibits the authority from issuing securities for such projects unless
16 the project was issued a certificate prior to January 1, 2020. This Part also restores the
17 authority of the Public Utilities Commission to issue a certificate of approval for an electric
18 rate stabilization agreement under the Electric Rate Reform Act by extending the date after
19 which no additional certificates may be issued from July 31, 1998 to July 31, 2040.
20 PART UU
21 This Part requires the State Controller to carry forward any unexpended balance
22 remaining at the end of fiscal year 2025-26 in the Maine Fire Protection Services
23 Commission, Maine Fire Protection Services Commission program, General Fund account,
24 All Other line category from a Public Law 2023, chapter 459 appropriation into the next
25 fiscal year to be used for fire service training facilities.
26 PART VV
27 This Part allows the Commissioner of Health and Human Services to accept donations
28 or grants for the Maine Child Care Affordability Program from any source and provides
29 that funds to support the program are nonlapsing.
30 PART WW
31 This Part requires the Department of Health and Human Services to establish
32 reimbursement rates for Maine Veterans' Homes nursing facility services covered by the
33 MaineCare program. It requires the MaineCare program to reimburse veterans' facilities
34 for covered nursing home services on a per resident daily rate basis that is reasonable and
35 adequate to cover the total MaineCare allowable costs of operating veterans' facilities. The
36 per diems developed must be adjusted for inflation to the same extent that reimbursement
37 rates for nonveterans' nursing facilities are adjusted.
38 This Part also requires the department to make a one-time supplemental payment to
39 Maine Veterans' Homes residential care facilities in fiscal year 2026-27 to cover the
40 difference between MaineCare payments and actual allowed MaineCare costs.
41 Lastly, this Part directs the department to use funds previously allocated or
42 appropriated for supplemental payments to Maine Veterans' Homes nursing facilities for
43 the purposes of this Part.

13 biennium.

17 appropriated for supplemental payments to psychiatric hospitals and the amount exceeding
18 federal payment limits.
19 PART BBB

22 not been addressed by some other Part without the necessity of finding that immediate
23 adoption is necessary to avoid a threat to public health, safety or welfare.

8 PART GGG
9 This Part repeals and replaces the existing statute regarding the fish hatchery
10 maintenance fund to provide that the fund must be used to support the engineering design,
11 construction, maintenance and repair of current and additional fish hatcheries and feeding
12 stations owned by the State and may be used to pay for overtime personnel services costs
13 for fish stocking and other hatchery-related work when operationally required. This Part
14 also requires that $2 from each fishing license sold be deposited into the fund and that
15 money deposited pursuant to the Maine Revised Statutes, Title 29-A, section 456-C,
16 subsection 3, paragraph A, subparagraph (1) must be credited to the fund.
17 PART HHH
18 This Part amends Public Law 2025, chapter 388, Part FF by increasing the amount of
19 funding that the Judicial Department is authorized to transfer in fiscal years 2025-26 and
20 2026-27 from available balances in the Personal Services line category to the All Other line
21 category within the General Fund from $750,000 to $1,250,000 in each year to fund
22 temporary clerk services, marshal services contracts, guardians ad litem, interpreters and
23 mental health services.
24 PART III
25 This Part authorizes the Judicial Department to transfer available Personal Services

31 Personal Services balances to the All Other line category in the Courts - Supreme, Superior
32 and District program, General Fund account to fund general facility improvements,
33 additional file storage costs and increased lease costs at the Lincoln District Court location.
34 PART JJJ
35 This Part amends the law establishing the Division for the Blind and Visually Impaired
36 to clarify its organizational structure, reflecting what has been in place since its creation
37 within the Department of Labor. The division is part of the Bureau of Rehabilitation
38 Services. The director of the division is not an appointee of the Commissioner of Labor.

11 PART NNN
12 This Part increases from 44 to 46 the number of judges the Governor must appoint to
13 the District Court.
14 PART OOO
15 This Part directs the Department of Public Safety to establish, operate and maintain a
16 sexual assault forensic examination kit tracking system. The system must provide relevant
17 information for victims, both those who choose to report to a law enforcement agency and
18 those who choose not to report, and other approved users regarding the processing, custody,
19 analysis and destruction of evidence. The department is required to submit an annual report
20 beginning January 1, 2028 to the joint standing committee of the Legislature having
21 jurisdiction over criminal justice and public safety matters and the Governor concerning
22 information related to the status of sexual assault forensic examination kits reported in the
23 tracking system. This Part provides requirements for the tracking system and requires the
24 department to adopt routine technical rules, including rules regarding participation in the
25 tracking system, confidentiality and the operation of the tracking system.
26 This Part directs that, by October 1, 2027, a law enforcement agency that receives,
27 maintains, stores or preserves sexual assault forensic examination kits must complete an
28 inventory of all kits in its possession and report its findings to the Department of Public
29 Safety. The department must compile all agency reports and present findings of the
30 inventory by January 1, 2028 to the joint standing committee of the Legislature having
31 jurisdiction over criminal justice and public safety matters and at the same time post the
32 report on the department's publicly accessible website.
33 Public Law 2023, chapter 236 changed a provision in the Maine Revised Statutes, Title
34 25 to require law enforcement agencies to store sexual assault forensic examination kits for
35 20 years. For consistency with that provision, this Part amends a provision in Title 24 to
36 require law enforcement agencies to store sexual assault forensic examination kits for 20
37 years.

1 This Part imposes an income tax surcharge of 2% on that portion of a taxpayer's Maine
2 taxable income in excess of $1,000,000 for single filers, $1,500,000 for heads of
3 households, $1,500,000 for married persons filing jointly or surviving spouses and 1/2 of

7 PART EEEE

10 electric ratepayer assistance are in addition to funds allocated for that purpose by the Public
